Which was probably a terrible idea because the lighting in there is just awful. They have those harsh fluorescent bulbs that buzz constantly and make everyone look kind of gray, you know? And the wall-mounted soap dispenser has been broken for months, so maintenance just left this giant pump bottle of cheap pink soap on the counter. It smells exactly like that artificial bubblegum flavor. Kind of like the liquid amoxicillin they give to kids? I always hated that smell. When I was little, I got strep throat like three times in one winter, and I had to take so much of that stuff. My mom would try to hide it by mixing it into my applesauce, but I could always tell because applesauce has that weird gritty texture if you really pay attention to it. We used to buy the big jars of it, but then my grandmother started making her own from the apple tree in her backyard... \n\nOh, wow, I'm sorry. I'm doing it again. I don't know why I can't just stay on track today. But um, yeah, to answer your question... about three weeks. Ever since right after I found out about the baby.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy levels or how quickly you're moving lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um, my energy is just... gone. Everything I do feels really slowed down, almost like I'm moving through syrup. Even simple things at the clinic, like gathering wound care supplies or just finishing up my charting, it's taking me noticeably longer than it should. \n\nSpeaking of charting, they actually just updated our electronic health record system last week, and it is a complete nightmare. They changed all the color codes for everything. The old system had patient allergies in bright red, which makes total sense, right? Red for danger. But now they're this weird pastel purple. Purple just reminds me of grape soda. I used to love grape soda when I was a kid. My grandpa would always buy those glass bottles of it from the corner store near his house. He had this old Ford pickup truck, and the seats were that sticky vinyl material that would absolutely burn the back of your legs in the summer if you were wearing shorts. We'd drive around with the windows down because the AC was broken, and his radio only ever picked up this one local country station that played the same five songs on a loop...\n\nOh, my goodness, I am so sorry. I'm doing it again. My brain is just everywhere. Um, but yeah, to answer your question, I just feel really sluggish. Like my body is just moving in slow motion lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange thoughts or heard any voices that others couldn't hear?\n\nPatient: No, no, nothing like that. I haven't heard any voices or had any weird thoughts. It's just the sadness and feeling so tired. \n\nHonestly, the only thing I've been hearing lately is my roommate's new blender. Sarah bought this massive, industrial-grade blender last weekend because she decided she's going to get really into making smoothie bowls. The thing sounds like a jet engine taking off in our kitchen. She uses it at like six in the morning, too. I tried to tell her that the walls in our apartment are paper thin, but she insists she needs to blend her frozen kale right then. Kale is so tough to blend anyway. I tried growing kale once on the balcony of my old apartment, but these little green caterpillars completely decimated it in a matter of days. I think they were cabbage loopers? I spent an entire afternoon reading about organic pest control, trying to figure out how to make a garlic spray to keep them away. I ended up making my whole apartment smell like a pizzeria, and the caterpillars didn't even care. They just kept eating...\n\nOh, shoot. I'm sorry. I really don't know why I keep doing this today. But no, to answer your question, no voices or anything strange like that. Just the blender.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or having trouble sitting still lately?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt restless. If anything, it's just the opposite, like I said, just feeling so slow and heavy. Sitting still is pretty much all I want to do right now. \n\nActually, I spent most of yesterday just sitting on my couch watching this documentary about deep-sea diving. It's crazy how dark it gets down there, and the pressure is just immense. They were talking about these little submersibles they use, and how the windows have to be made of this special thick acrylic. Acrylic is such a weird material when you think about it. We used to use acrylic paints in my high school art class. Mr. Henderson, my art teacher, was obsessed with painting bowls of fruit. Like, every single week it was a new bowl of fruit. He had this fake plastic fruit that he'd arrange on a velvet cloth, but the plastic banana had this huge dent in it from where someone dropped it, and he refused to replace it. He said it added character. He was a funny guy, always wore these brightly patterned bow ties. I think he had a different one for every day of the month...\n\nUgh, I'm doing it again, aren't I? I am so sorry. My mind just keeps wandering off. But no, I'm not restless. I'm not having any trouble sitting still.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, I think I kind of mentioned it earlier, but yeah, I definitely feel like I'm moving a lot slower. Everything just takes so much effort. Even my speech feels a bit... sluggish, I guess. Like it takes me a minute to find the right words. \n\nSpeaking of words, I was trying to do a crossword puzzle the other day to keep my mind sharp, you know? But the clues were so obscure. One was a five-letter word for a type of ancient Greek coin. Who knows that off the top of their head? My brother used to collect coins when we were little. He had this big, heavy album filled with quarters from all fifty states. He was so meticulous about it, always polishing them and organizing them by year. He got really upset once because I accidentally spent his rare 1976 Bicentennial quarter on a candy bar at the gas station. Like, after my shifts at the clinic, I used to always paint, or I'd go to my yoga class, or sometimes even salsa dancing just to decompress. But lately, I just go straight home, sit on that same couch, and ignore all my texts because none of it feels worth the effort. It's like the color has been drained out of everything. \n\nSpeaking of color, I bought these really expensive watercolor paints a few months ago. They're from this boutique brand in France that still grinds their own pigments by hand. The ultramarine blue is supposed to be incredible, made from real lapis lazuli, just like they did in the Renaissance. I read this whole book once about the history of the color blue. Did you know it was one of the last colors to have a word for it in most ancient languages? Homer used to describe the sea as \"wine-dark\" instead of blue in The Odyssey. I had to read The Odyssey in my freshman year lit class, and my professor was this really intense guy who would pace back and forth across the front of the lecture hall, gesturing wildly. He actually tripped over a projector cord once and took down the whole screen with him. It made this massive crashing sound, and...\n\nOh, goodness. I'm sorry. I really can't seem to stay on topic today. But yeah, no, I don't think I'd enjoy those things right now, even if I wasn't so tired. I just don't care about them anymore.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no. Like I was just saying a second ago, all those things I used to do to decompress after my shifts—the painting, the yoga, the salsa dancing—I just don't have any interest in them anymore. I really just go home, sit on the couch, and ignore all my texts. \n\nI have so many unread messages right now. My friend Jenna keeps texting me pictures of her new puppy. She actually helped design a mobile app for tracking local food trucks. It was a pretty cool idea, you could see exactly where the taco truck or the crepe stand was parked in real time. I just feel incredibly slow and weighed down.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or restless when you're not doing anything?\n\nPatient: I feel like I just answered that, but no, I'm really not restless or fidgety. It's totally the opposite. Like I was saying, everything I do just feels so slowed down. It's like I'm moving through syrup all the time. Even the most basic stuff at the clinic, like grabbing wound care supplies or trying to finish my charting, it takes me so much longer than it used to. \n\nWound care can be so tedious anyway, especially when you have to pack a deep wound. I was mentioning earlier that I just ignore all my texts now, even from my close friends. I'm just pulling away from everyone and everything that usually keeps me steady. I'm still making it to work at the clinic, but barely. She reminded me so much of my middle school librarian, Mrs. Higgins. Mrs. Higgins was obsessed with the Dewey Decimal System. I just don't have the energy for people right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself pacing back and forth or feeling an inner restlessness when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: I feel like we just talked about this, but no, I'm not pacing or restless at all. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I'm honestly not sure if this is the absolute worst it's ever been. It's hard to compare, you know? It's just really bad right now. I've definitely felt down before, but this just feels so heavy. Like I said, everything is just so slow. It's like that feeling when you're trying to run in a dream and you just can't get your legs to move fast enough.

It rains so much out there. I don't think I could handle that much rain. I need the sun, even if I'm just sitting inside avoiding everyone right now. I used to love sitting out on the porch in the summer with a glass of iced tea, the real kind, brewed with lots of sugar, not that powdered stuff. The powdered stuff always clumps up at the bottom of the pitcher and you have to stir it forever...

Wait, what was the question? Oh, right. Have I felt this low before? I don't know, maybe? It's just really awful right now.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I know you just asked me this, or something really similar. But no, no fidgeting. It's really the complete opposite. Everything I do feels so slowed down. Like I'm moving through syrup. The AC was totally busted, so in the summer I'd have to roll all the windows down, and my hair would get so tangled it took half a bottle of conditioner just to brush it out... 

Sorry, I'm doing it again, aren't I? But no, no fidgeting. Just really, really slow.

I don't know about things improving. I just... I came in because for the past few weeks, I’ve felt this heavy, sad pressure sitting on me almost all day. Even when I’m at work, smiling at the patients and pretending everything is fine, inside I feel like I’m barely holding myself together. It’s exhausting, honestly. 

Smiling takes so much energy. We have this one patient, Mrs. Gable, she comes in every Tuesday for her B12 shot, and she always brings us these homemade snickerdoodles. They are amazing, honestly, the perfect amount of cinnamon. My grandmother used to make snickerdoodles, but hers were always a little too hard. She baked them on this ancient, warped baking sheet that she refused to throw away. She said it seasoned the cookies, whatever that means. It was black with years of burnt sugar and grease. She kept it in the drawer under the oven, the one where everyone else keeps their extra pots and pans. I don't actually have that drawer in my apartment right now. The kitchen is so tiny, you can barely open the fridge all the way without hitting the counter. It’s one of those old buildings with the exposed brick, which looks cool, but it’s drafty in the winter. The heating bill was outrageous last January. I had to buy one of those space heaters that looks like a little fireplace. It even has fake flames that flicker... 

But yeah, I don't know if I can imagine feeling better. Right now, it's just that constant, heavy sadness all day long.

The cafeteria food in middle school was actually pretty decent, they had these amazing square pizzas on Fridays. The cheese would get all crispy around the edges...\n\nOh, sorry. I don't know why I keep doing that. But to answer your question, yes, I'm definitely avoiding social interactions. I just don't have the energy for people right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself pacing back and forth or feeling an inner restlessness when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: I feel like we just talked about this, but no, I'm not pacing or restless at all. It's the exact opposite, like I've been saying. Everything I do just feels so incredibly slowed down. It's like I'm moving through syrup, just to do the most basic things. Even at the clinic, just gathering wound supplies or trying to finish my charting, it takes me noticeably longer than it should. \n\nAnd I know I keep bringing up the charting, but it really is the worst. We have this one doctor, Dr. Aris, who is so particular about how the notes are formatted. He wants everything in this very specific order, and if you miss one little detail, he sends it back to you. He's a good doctor, I guess, but he has zero bedside manner. He actually wears these ridiculous bow ties every single day. I don't think I've ever seen him in a regular tie. My grandfather used to wear bow ties, but he was a professor of medieval history, so it kind of fit his whole vibe, you know? He had this massive library in his house, floor-to-ceiling bookshelves filled with these dusty old tomes. I used to love the smell of that room. It smelled like old paper and pipe tobacco. He smoked cherry pipe tobacco, which I know is terrible for you, but it smelled so sweet. I tried to find a candle that smelled like that once, but they all just smelled artificial, kind of like that cheap chocolate I was talking about earlier. It's so hard to find a good candle that actually smells like what it says it does. \n\nOh, gosh. I'm sorry. I'm totally off track again. But yeah, no inner restlessness or pacing. Just feeling really, really slow and heavy.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current state is the worst it's been, or have you felt this low before?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure if this is the absolute worst it's ever been. It's hard to compare, you know? It's just really bad right now. I've definitely felt down before, but this just feels so heavy. Like I said, everything is just so slow. It's like that feeling when you're trying to run in a dream and you just can't get your legs to move fast enough. \n\nI actually used to have this recurring dream when I was a kid where I was trying to run away from a giant wave, but my feet were stuck in the sand. We went to the beach every summer in North Carolina, the Outer Banks. The sand there gets so incredibly hot by mid-afternoon, you have to sprint from your towel to the water or your feet will literally blister. My brother actually got second-degree burns on his soles once because he refused to wear his flip-flops. He was always so stubborn. He’s an engineer now, building bridges or something out in Oregon. It rains so much out there. I don't think I could handle that much rain. I need the sun, even if I'm just sitting inside avoiding everyone right now. I used to love sitting out on the porch in the summer with a glass of iced tea, the real kind, brewed with lots of sugar, not that powdered stuff. Father Thomas was the one who hid them, and he was surprisingly spry for a guy in his seventies. He used to ride this vintage Schwinn bicycle all over town. It was that classic robin's egg blue color. My first car was actually that same color, a hand-me-down Honda Civic.
